Is there any way to make GPU output hash match original while maintaining high performance? Maybe use a deterministic sequential accumulation but with a coalesced parallel algorithm that sums in original order? There is a technique: each row processed by a warp, but lane 0 combines products sequentially from a shared buffer filled in parallel. That would require storing 32 products and having lane 0 sum them in order, serializing 32 adds but parallelizing multiply/load. For rows of 102 nnz, lane 0 sums ~102 in order, losing parallelism in adds but memory loads parallel. Then lane 0 adds sequentially with DADD. This yields bitwise identical to CPU! And memory bound, so performance may still be decent: warp loads 32 elements at a time (coalesced), each lane computes product, stores to shared, lane 0 accumulates in order. But shared memory round-trip and serialization each 32-chunk add. Still, the bandwidth is coalesced (good), and adds are serial but cheap relative to memory.
